- **react18-commander**: Master orchestrator for React 16/17 → 18.3.1 migration. Designed for class-component-heavy codebases. Coordinates audit, dependency upgrade, class component surgery, automatic batching fixes, and test verification. Uses memory to gate each phase and resume interrupted sessions. 18.3.1 is the target - it surface-exposes every deprecation that React 19 will remove, so the output is a codebase ready for the React 19 orchestra next. ([brycewang-stanford/Auto-Empirical-Research-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Auto-Empirical-Research-Skills/blob/HEAD/agents/README.md) · ★3.9k)
- **README**: This directory contains behavioral definitions for specialized agents used in the research workflow. Unlike skills (which provide domain knowledge), agents define **behavioral protocols** for specific roles. ([brycewang-stanford/Auto-Empirical-Research-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Auto-Empirical-Research-Skills/blob/HEAD/skills/17-DAAF-Contribution-Community-daaf/dot-claude/agents/README.md) · ★3.9k · 3 copies)
- **README**: Default agent instruction files copied into a new workspace. They define the meeting and meeting-UI agent roles available to workspace routines. ([Vexa-ai/vexa](https://github.com/Vexa-ai/vexa/blob/HEAD/core/agent/workspace-seeds/default/agents/README.md) · ★2.8k · 4 copies)
- **README**: Named, domain-specialized agents that the Hyperflow chain dispatches. Each is a real Claude Code sub-agent (`agents/&lt;name&gt;.md`, auto-discovered by the plugin) with frontmatter (`name`, `description`, `tools`) and a charter body. Every specialist runs on the current session model — there is no `model:` field. ([jeremylongshore/tons-of-skills-marketplace](https://github.com/jeremylongshore/tons-of-skills-marketplace/blob/HEAD/plugins/ai-agency/hyperflow/agents/README.md) · ★2.8k)
- **README**: Everything an AI coding agent needs that isn't tied to one vendor lives here, so Claude Code, Codex and anything else read the same material. ([TrackerControl/tracker-control-android](https://github.com/TrackerControl/tracker-control-android/blob/HEAD/agents/README.md) · ★2.7k)
- **README**: All agents live here under a single flat taxonomy. There is no `core/` layer and no `catalog/` layer — every agent follows the same schema and is discoverable through the generated index. ([GammaLabTechnologies/harmonist](https://github.com/GammaLabTechnologies/harmonist/blob/HEAD/agents/README.md) · ★2.3k)
- **README**: mcp-memory-service provides persistent shared memory for multi-agent systems via a **framework-agnostic REST API**. No MCP client library required — any HTTP client works. ([doobidoo/mcp-memory-service](https://github.com/doobidoo/mcp-memory-service/blob/HEAD/docs/agents/README.md) · ★2k)
- **README**: This directory captures the docs-only specification for adding agent orchestration to Kradle git workspaces. The focus is a system-wide orchestration model: define agent stacks, bind tools/skills/subagents, connect them to triggers, run them on policy-controlled workspaces, and present each dispatch like a CI pipeline run with live Agent Adapter chat/session access. ([a5c-ai/babysitter](https://github.com/a5c-ai/babysitter/blob/HEAD/packages/kradle/core/docs/agents/README.md) · ★1.8k)

- **README**: Standalone agent packages for the GAIA Agent Hub. Each agent gets one directory with a runtime subdir inside — `hub/agents/&lt;id&gt;/python/` for the Python wheel, `hub/agents/&lt;id&gt;/npm/` for a JS/TS client, `hub/agents/&lt;id&gt;/cpp/` for a native build. Each package depends on the published `amd-gaia` framework — third-party contributors follow the exact same pattern AMD uses for its own agents. ([amd/gaia](https://github.com/amd/gaia/blob/HEAD/hub/agents/README.md) · ★1.6k · 2 copies)
- **README**: Each subdirectory here holds a `gaia-agent.yaml` packaging manifest for a native (C++) agent. ([gagan114662/agent-skills](https://github.com/gagan114662/agent-skills/blob/HEAD/platform/agents/README.md))
